static void azx_update_rirb(struct azx *chip)\n830: {\n831: \tunsigned int rp, wp;\n832: \tunsigned int addr;\n833: \tu32 res, res_ex;\n834: \n835: \twp = azx_readb(chip, RIRBWP);\n836: \tif (wp == 0;\n2077: }\n2078: \n2079: /* get the current DMA position with correction on VIA chips */\n2080: static unsigned int azx_via_get_position(struct azx *chip,\n2081: \t\t\t\t\t struct azx_dev *azx_dev)\n2082: {\n2083: \tunsigned int link_pos, mini_pos, bound_pos;\n2084: \tunsigned int mod_link_pos, mod_dma_pos, mod_mini_pos;\n2085: \tunsigned int fifo_size;\n2086: \n2087: \tlink_pos = azx_dev->period_bytes;\n2120: \tmod_link_pos = link_pos % azx_dev->period_bytes;\n2121: \tif (mod_link_pos >= fifo_size)\n2122: \t\tbound_pos = link_pos - mod_link_pos;\n2123: \telse if (mod_dma_pos azx_get_position(struct azx *chip,\n2136: \t\t\t\t     struct azx_dev *azx_dev,\n2137: \t\t\t\t     bool with_check)\n2138: {\n2139: \tunsigned int pos;\n2140: \tint stream = azx_dev->substream->stream;\n2141: \n2142: \tswitch (chip->position_fix[stream]) {\n2143: \tcase POS_FIX_LPIB:\n2144: \t\t/* read LPIB */\n2145: \t\tpos = azx_sd_readl(azx_dev, SD_LPIB);\n2146: bytes_to_frames(substream->runtime,\n2201: \t\t\t       azx_get_position(chip, azx_dev, false));\n2202: }\n2203: \n2204: /*\n2205:  * Check whether the current DMA position is acceptable for updating\n2206:  * periods.  Returns non-zero if it's OK.\n2207:  *\n2208:  * Many HD-audio controllers appear pretty inaccurate about\n2209:  * the update-IRQ timing.  The IRQ is issued before actually the\n2210:  * data is processed.  So, we need to process it afterwords in a\n2211:  * workqueue.\n2212:  */\n2213: static int azx_position_ok(struct azx *chip, struct azx_dev *azx_dev)\n2214: {\n2215: \tu32 wallclk;\n2216: \tunsigned int pos;\n2217: \tint stream;\n2218: \n2219: \twallclk = azx_readl(chip, WALLCLK) - azx_dev->start_wallclk;\n2220: \tif (wallclk < (azx_dev->period_wallclk * 2) / 3)\n2221: \t\treturn -1;\t/* bogus (too early) interrupt */\n2222: \n2223: \tstream = azx_dev->substream->stream;\n2224: \tpos = azx_get_position(chip, azx_dev, true);\n2225: \n2226: \tif (WARN_ONCE(!azx_dev->period_bytes,\n2227: \t\t      \"hda-intel: zero azx_dev->period_bytes\"))\n2228: \t\treturn -1; /* this shouldn't happen! */\n2229: \tif (wallclk < (azx_dev->period_wallclk * 5) / 4 &&\n2230: \t    pos % azx_dev->period_bytes > azx_dev->period_bytes / 2)\n2231: \t\t/* NG - it's below the first next period boundary */\n2232: \t\treturn bdl_pos_adj[chip->dev_index] ? 0 : -1;\n2233: \tazx_dev->start_wallclk += wallclk;\n2234: \treturn 1; /* OK, it's fine */\n2235: }\n2236: \n2237: /*\n2238:  * The work for pending PCM period updates.\n2239:  */\n2240: static void azx_irq_pending_work(struct work_struct *work)\n2241: {\n2242: \tstruct azx *chip = container_of(work, struct azx, irq_pending_work);\n2243: \tint i, pending, ok;\n2244: \n2245: \tif (!chip->irq_pending_warned) {\n2246: \t\tprintk(KERN_WARNING\n2247: \t\t       \"hda-intel: IRQ timing workaround is activated \"\n2248: \t\t       \"for card #%d. So if these were set to 1 before suspend, they might remain set even after chip->initialized is reset.\n\nHowever, looking at azx_init_chip:\n- If chip->initialized is 0, it will run through the full initialization\n\n\n- It checks `if (!chip->single_cmd)` on line 1222 before initializing command I/O\n- If single_cmd is 1, command I/O won't be initialized\n\nSo the bug is: if a timeout sets `chip->single_cmd = 1`, suspend clears `chip->initialized` but leaves `single_cmd` set. Now let me look for any code that might not be resetting these fields during resume.Looking at the full picture, the bug is clear:\n\n1. During error handling in azx_get_response, three flags can be set:\n   - chip->polling_mode = 1\n   - chip->single_cmd = 1\n   - chip->msi = 0\n\n2. These flags are used to control how the driver operates, but they're NOT reset on resume\n\n3. Specifically, when azx_init_chip is called on resume, it checks `if (!chip->single_cmd)` before initializing command I/O. If single_cmd was set during an error, it won't be reinitialized, leaving the driver in a degraded state.\n\n4. This gets worse each time there's a timeout, especially if polling_mode was enabled, since the timeout threshold is lowered (more likely to timeout again).\n\nThe fix should be to reset these error state flags during resume. Now I can see the full picture. `azx_init_cmd_io` initializes the Command Output Ring Buffer (CORB) and Response Input Ring Buffer (RIRB) - the hardware command interfaces. When azx_get_response encounters repeated timeouts, it sets `chip->single_cmd = 1` and calls `azx_free_cmd_io` to disable CORB/RIRB2.  /* Initialize CORB/RIRB buffers */\n   ```\n   If `chip->single_cmd` is still 1 from a previous error, the critical Command Output Ring Buffer (CORB) and Response Input Ring Buffer (RIRB) are **not reinitialized**, leaving the hardware command interface in an uninitialized state.\n\n4. **Result**: The codec becomes unresponsive after resume, causing choppy audio as the driver falls back to degraded communication modes.\n\nThe fault localization result has been written to `/workspace/fault_localization_result.json`."}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":4,"cache_creation_input_tokens":412,"cache_read_input_tokens":59996,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":0,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":412},"output_tokens":1,"service_tier":"standard","inference_geo":"not_available"},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"fc86b5cd-5350-4204-b2ea-ec737aa6127d","uuid":"14ee7543-b13f-4b77-bc5a-5398dd4035cb"}
